# Claims to review

UNSUPPORTED (p < 0.5), NUMBER_MISMATCH (a number is not in the paper) and DOUBTFUL (0.5 <= p < 0.85). On the gold set no UNSUPPORTED claim was true, but 7 of 24 DOUBTFUL ones were false.

  - claim: First, it introduces segment-level recurrence with state reuse: during training, hidden states from the previous segment are cached with stop-gradient and concatenated with the current segment's hidden states as an extended context; keys and values are computed from this extended context, while queries are computed from the current segment.
  - paper: Dur- ing training, the hidden state sequence computed for the previous segment is fixed and cached to be reused as an extended context when the model processes the next new segment, as shown in Fig. 2a.

- **c4744a7c_Transformer-XL Attent** [problem_addressed] p=0.65
  - claim: Transformer-based language models are limited by fixed-length training contexts and arbitrary segment boundaries, which prevents them from modeling long-term dependencies, causes context fragmentation, and makes vanilla Transformer evaluation slow because each new prediction position requires recomputing the context from scratch.
  - paper: During evaluation, at each step, the vanilla model also consumes a segment of the same length as in training, but only makes one prediction at the last position.
  - paper: Then, at the next step, the segment is shifted to the right by only one position, and the new segment has to be processed all from scratch.

- **c4744a7c_Transformer-XL Attent** [methodology] p=0.82
  - claim: This reparameterizes attention into content-based addressing, content-dependent positional bias, global content bias, and global positional bias, making state reuse coherent and allowing the model to generalize to longer evaluation contexts.
  - paper: Under the new parameterization, each term has an intuitive meaning: term (a) represents content- based addressing, term (b) captures a content- dependent positional bias, term (c) governs a global content bias, and (d) encodes a global po- sitional bias.
  - paper: As a benefit of the inductive bias, a model trained on a memory of some certain length can automatically generalize to a memory several times longer during evaluation.
